```
1→import { normalizeAndPresentError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandling/runtimeError';
     2→import {
     3→  getPendingJoinClipsCandidateKeys,
     4→  getPendingJoinClipsStorageKey,
     5→  isPendingJoinClipInScope,
     6→  type PendingJoinClipEntry,
     7→} from '@/shared/lib/joinClipsPendingQueue';
     8→import { consumeJoinClipsIntents } from '@/shared/lib/joinClipsIntentStore';
     9→import { generateUUID } from '@/shared/lib/taskCreation';
    10→import { readUserIdFromStorage } from '@/shared/lib/supabaseSession';
    11→import {
    12→  operationFailure,
    13→  operationSuccess,
    14→  type OperationResult,
    15→} from '@/shared/lib/operationResult';
    16→import type { VideoClip } from '../types';
    17→
    18→const PENDING_CLIPS_TTL_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000;
    19→
    20→interface PendingJoinClipParseResult {
    21→  entries: PendingJoinClipEntry[];
    22→  invalidCount: number;
    23→}
    24→
    25→function parsePendingJoinClips(raw: string): PendingJoinClipParseResult | null {
    26→  let parsed: unknown;
    27→  try {
    28→    parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    29→  } catch {
    30→    return null;
    31→  }
    32→
    33→  if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) {
    34→    return null;
    35→  }
    36→
    37→  const entries: PendingJoinClipEntry[] = [];
    38→  let invalidCount = 0;
    39→  for (const value of parsed) {
    40→    if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)) {
    41→      invalidCount += 1;
    42→      continue;
    43→    }
    44→
    45→    const candidate = value as Record<string, unknown>;
    46→    if (
    47→      typeof candidate.videoUrl !== 'string'
    48→      || typeof candidate.generationId !== 'string'
    49→      || typeof candidate.timestamp !== 'number'
    50→      || !Number.isFinite(candidate.timestamp)
    51→    ) {
    52→      invalidCount += 1;
    53→      continue;
    54→    }
    55→
    56→    entries.push({
    57→      videoUrl: candidate.videoUrl,
    58→      generationId: candidate.generationId,
    59→      timestamp: candidate.timestamp,
    60→      ...(typeof candidate.thumbnailUrl === 'string' && candidate.thumbnailUrl.length > 0
    61→        ? { thumbnailUrl: candidate.thumbnailUrl }
    62→        : {}),
    63→      ...(typeof candidate.projectId === 'string' && candidate.projectId.length > 0
    64→        ? { projectId: candidate.projectId }
    65→        : {}),
    66→      ...(typeof candidate.userId === 'string' && candidate.userId.length > 0
    67→        ? { userId: candidate.userId }
    68→        : {}),
    69→    });
    70→  }
    71→
    72→  return { entries, invalidCount };
    73→}
    74→
    75→/** Create a throwaway <video> to read duration from a URL. */
    76→function getVideoDurationFromUrl(videoUrl: string): Promise<number> {
    77→  return new Promise<number>(resolve => {
    78→    const videoElement = document.createElement('video');
    79→    videoElement.preload = 'metadata';
    80→    videoElement.onloadedmetadata = () => resolve(videoElement.duration);
    81→    videoElement.onerror = () => resolve(0);
    82→    videoElement.src = videoUrl;
    83→  });
    84→}
    85→
    86→/**
    87→ * Reads and consumes pending join clips from localStorage.
    88→ * Returns deferred actions that are applied to clip state later.
    89→ * Placement is intentionally not resolved here because only the consumer has
    90→ * the previous clip array needed to choose fill-vs-append.
    91→ */
    92→export type PendingClipAction = {
    93→  type: 'deferred_insert';
    94→  clip: VideoClip;
    95→};
    96→
    97→interface ConsumePendingJoinClipsOptions {
    98→  projectId?: string | null;
    99→  readVideoDuration?: (videoUrl: string) => Promise<number>;
   100→}
   101→
   102→async function toPendingClipActions(
   103→  entries: PendingJoinClipEntry[],
   104→  readVideoDuration: (videoUrl: string) => Promise<number>,
   105→): Promise<PendingClipAction[]> {
   106→  const actions: PendingClipAction[] = [];
   107→
   108→  for (const { videoUrl, thumbnailUrl, generationId } of entries) {
   109→    if (!videoUrl) continue;
   110→
   111→    const durationSeconds = await readVideoDuration(videoUrl);
   112→
   113→    const clip: VideoClip = {
   114→      id: generateUUID(),
   115→      url: videoUrl,
   116→      posterUrl: thumbnailUrl,
   117→      durationSeconds,
   118→      loaded: false,
   119→      playing: false,
   120→      generationId,
   121→    };
   122→
   123→    actions.push({ type: 'deferred_insert', clip });
   124→  }
   125→
   126→  return actions;
   127→}
   128→
   129→export async function consumePendingJoinClips(
   130→  options: ConsumePendingJoinClipsOptions = {},
   131→): Promise<OperationResult<PendingClipAction[]>> {
   132→  const projectId = options.projectId ?? null;
   133→  const readVideoDuration = options.readVideoDuration ?? getVideoDurationFromUrl;
   134→
   135→  try {
   136→    const userId = readUserIdFromStorage();
   137→
   138→    const inMemoryPending = consumeJoinClipsIntents({ projectId, userId });
   139→    if (inMemoryPending.length > 0) {
   140→      const now = Date.now();
   141→      const scopedRecentClips = inMemoryPending.filter(
   142→        (clip) =>
   143→          now - clip.timestamp < PENDING_CLIPS_TTL_MS
   144→          && isPendingJoinClipInScope(clip, { projectId, userId }),
   145→      );
   146→      if (scopedRecentClips.length === 0) {
   147→        localStorage.removeItem(getPendingJoinClipsStorageKey(projectId, userId));
   148→        return operationSuccess([]);
   149→      }
   150→
   151→      const actions = await toPendingClipActions(scopedRecentClips, readVideoDuration);
   152→      localStorage.removeItem(getPendingJoinClipsStorageKey(projectId, userId));
   153→      return operationSuccess(actions);
   154→    }
   155→
   156→    const pendingKeys = getPendingJoinClipsCandidateKeys({ projectId, userId });
   157→    const pendingSource = pendingKeys
   158→      .map((key) => ({ key, raw: localStorage.getItem(key) }))
   159→      .find((item) => typeof item.raw === 'string' && item.raw.length > 0);
   160→
   161→    if (!pendingSource || !pendingSource.raw) {
   162→      return operationSuccess([]);
   163→    }
   164→
   165→    const parsedPendingClips = parsePendingJoinClips(pendingSource.raw);
   166→    if (!parsedPendingClips) {
   167→      normalizeAndPresentError(new Error('Invalid pending join clips payload'), {
   168→        context: 'JoinClipsPage.pendingClipsInvalid',
   169→        showToast: false,
   170→        logData: { [REDACTED] },
   171→      });
   172→      localStorage.removeItem(pendingSource.key);
   173→      return operationFailure(new Error('Invalid pending join clips payload'), {
   174→        policy: 'degrade',
   175→        errorCode: 'pending_join_clips_invalid_payload',
   176→        message: 'Invalid pending join clips payload',
   177→        recoverable: true,
   178→        cause: { [REDACTED] },
   179→      });
   180→    }
   181→
   182→    if (parsedPendingClips.invalidCount > 0) {
   183→      normalizeAndPresentError(new Error('Dropped invalid pending join clip entries'), {
   184→        context: 'JoinClipsPage.pendingClipsPartialRecovery',
   185→        showToast: false,
   186→        logData: {
   187→          [REDACTED],
   188→          totalEntries: parsedPendingClips.entries.length + parsedPendingClips.invalidCount,
   189→          invalidEntries: parsedPendingClips.invalidCount,
   190→        },
   191→      });
   192→    }
   193→
   194→    const now = Date.now();
   195→    const recentClips = parsedPendingClips.entries.filter(
   196→      clip => now - clip.timestamp < PENDING_CLIPS_TTL_MS,
   197→    );
   198→    const scopedRecentClips = recentClips.filter((clip) =>
   199→      isPendingJoinClipInScope(clip, { projectId, userId }),
   200→    );
   201→
   202→    if (scopedRecentClips.length === 0) {
   203→      localStorage.removeItem(pendingSource.key);
   204→      return operationSuccess([], {
   205→        ...(parsedPendingClips.invalidCount > 0 ? { policy: 'degrade' } : {}),
   206→      });
   207→    }
   208→
   209→    const actions = await toPendingClipActions(scopedRecentClips, readVideoDuration);
   210→
   211→    localStorage.removeItem(pendingSource.key);
   212→    return operationSuccess(actions, {
   213→      ...(parsedPendingClips.invalidCount > 0 ? { policy: 'degrade' } : {}),
   214→    });
   215→  } catch (error) {
   216→    normalizeAndPresentError(error, { context: 'JoinClipsPage', showToast: false });
   217→    return operationFailure(error, {
   218→      policy: 'degrade',
   219→      errorCode: 'pending_join_clips_read_failed',
   220→      message: 'Failed to consume pending join clips',
   221→      recoverable: true,
   222→      cause: error,
   223→    });
   224→  }
   225→}
   226→
   227→/**
   228→ * Apply pending clip actions to an existing clips array.
   229→ * Resolves each deferred action by filling the first available empty slot,
   230→ * or appending when no empty slots remain.
   231→ */
   232→export function applyPendingClipActions(
   233→  prevClips: VideoClip[],
   234→  actions: PendingClipAction[],
   235→): VideoClip[] {
   236→  let clips = [...prevClips];
   237→  // Track which indices have been filled by previous actions in this batch
   238→  const filledIndices = new Set<number>();
   239→
   240→  for (const action of actions) {
   241→    const emptyIndex = clips.findIndex(
   242→      (clip, idx) => !clip.url && !filledIndices.has(idx),
   243→    );
   244→
   245→    if (emptyIndex !== -1) {
   246→      clips = clips.map((clip, idx) =>
   247→        idx === emptyIndex
   248→          ? {
   249→              ...clip,
   250→              url: action.clip.url,
   251→              posterUrl: action.clip.posterUrl,
   252→              durationSeconds: action.clip.durationSeconds,
```

```
     3→type FreshnessSubscriber = () => void;

     5→export class DataFreshnessSubscribers {
```

```
import { useShotEditorLayoutModel } from './controllers/useShotEditorLayoutModel';
import { SETTINGS_IDS } from '@/shared/lib/settingsIds';

interface ShotEditorControllerResult {
  hasSelectedShot: boolean;
  layoutProps: ShotEditorLayoutProps;
}

export function useShotEditorController({
  selectedShotId,
  projectId,
  optimisticShotData,
  onShotImagesUpdate,
  onBack,
  dimensionSource,
  onDimensionSourceChange,
  customWidth,
  onCustomWidthChange,
  customHeight,
  onCustomHeightChange,
  onPreviousShot,
  onNextShot,
  hasPrevious,
  hasNext,
  onUpdateShotName,
  getFinalVideoCount,
  getHasStructureVideo,
  headerContainerRef: parentHeaderRef,
  timelineSectionRef: parentTimelineRef,
  ctaContainerRef: parentCtaRef,
  onSelectionChange: parentOnSelectionChange,
  getGenerationDataRef: parentGetGenerationDataRef,
  generateVideoRef: parentGenerateVideoRef,
  nameClickRef: parentNameClickRef,
  isSticky,
  variantName: parentVariantName,
  onVariantNameChange: parentOnVariantNameChange,
  isGeneratingVideo: parentIsGeneratingVideo,
  videoJustQueued: parentVideoJustQueued,
  onDragStateChange,
}: ShotEditorProps): ShotEditorControllerResult {
  const promptSettings = usePromptSettings();
  const motionSettings = useMotionSettings();
  const frameSettings = useFrameSettings();
  const phaseConfigSettings = usePhaseConfigSettings();
  const generationModeSettings = useGenerationModeSettings();
  const steerableMotionSettingsFromContext = useSteerableMotionSettings();
  const loraSettingsFromContext = useLoraSettings();
  const { isLoading: settingsLoadingFromContext } = useVideoTravelSettings();

  const {
    selectedShot,
    shots,
    selectedProjectId,
    projects,
    effectiveAspectRatio,
    allShotImages,
    timelineImages,
    unpositionedImages,
    videoOutputs,
    contextImages,
    initialParentGenerations,
    refs: { selectedShotRef, projectIdRef, allShotImagesRef, batchVideoFramesRef },
  } = useShotEditorSetup({
    selectedShotId,
    projectId,
    optimisticShotData: optimisticShotData as Shot | undefined,
    batchVideoFrames: frameSettings.batchVideoFrames,
  });

  const queryClient = useQueryClient();
  const { setCurrentShotId } = useCurrentShot();
  const { navigateToShot } = useShotNavigation();
  const { createShot } = useShotCreation();
  const { mutateAsync: addToShotMutation } = useAddImageToShot();
  const { mutateAsync: addToShotWithoutPositionMutation } = useAddImageToShotWithoutPosition();

  const createShotRef = useRef(createShot);
  createShotRef.current = createShot;
  const addToShotMutationRef = useRef(addToShotMutation);
  addToShotMutationRef.current = addToShotMutation;
  const addToShotWithoutPositionMutationRef = useRef(addToShotWithoutPositionMutation);
  addToShotWithoutPositionMutationRef.current = addToShotWithoutPositionMutation;

  const handleDragStateChange = useCallback((isDragging: boolean) => {
    onDragStateChange?.(isDragging);
  }, [onDragStateChange]);

  const lastVideoGeneration = useLastVideoGeneration(selectedShotId);

  const updateShotImageOrderMutation = useUpdateShotImageOrder();
  const {
    settings: shotUISettings,
    update: updateShotUISettings,
    isLoading: isShotUISettingsLoading
  } = useToolSettings<{
    acceleratedMode?: boolean;
    randomSeed?: boolean;
  }>(SETTINGS_IDS.TRAVEL_UI_STATE, {
    projectId: selectedProjectId,
    shotId: selectedShot?.id,
    enabled: !!selectedShot?.id
  });

  const isMobile = useIsMobile();
  const { isPhone, aspectAdjustedColumns } = useAspectAdjustedColumns(effectiveAspectRatio);
  const { setIsGenerationsPaneLocked } = usePanes();

  const { update: updateShotGenerationsPaneSettings } = useToolSettings<GenerationsPaneSettings>(SETTINGS_IDS.GENERATIONS_PANE, {
    shotId: selectedShotId,
    enabled: !!selectedShotId
  });

  const { state, actions } = useShotEditorState();

  const setIsGenerationsPaneLockedRef = useRef(setIsGenerationsPaneLocked);
  setIsGenerationsPaneLockedRef.current = setIsGenerationsPaneLocked;
  const actionsRef = useRef(actions);
  actionsRef.current = actions;
  const updateShotGenerationsPaneSettingsRef = useRef(updateShotGenerationsPaneSettings);
  updateShotGenerationsPaneSettingsRef.current = updateShotGenerationsPaneSettings;

  const centerSectionRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const videoGalleryRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const generateVideosCardRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const joinSegmentsSectionRef = useRef<HTMLDivElement>(null);
  const swapButtonRef = useRef<HTMLButtonElement>(null);

  const { loraManager } = useLoraSync({
    selectedLoras: loraSettingsFromContext.selectedLoras,
    onSelectedLorasChange: loraSettingsFromContext.setSelectedLoras,
    projectId: selectedProjectId,
    availableLoras: loraSettingsFromContext.availableLoras,
    batchVideoPrompt: promptSettings.prompt,
    onBatchVideoPromptChange: promptSettings.setPrompt,
  });
  const isShotLoraSettingsLoading = false;

  const { output, editing } = useShotEditorMediaAndOutputControllers({
    selectedProjectId,
    selectedShotId,
    selectedShot: selectedShot ?? null,
    projectId,
    timelineImages,
    effectiveAspectRatio,
    swapButtonRef,
    onUpdateShotName,
    state: { isEditingName: state.isEditingName, editingName: state.editingName },
    actions,
    generationTypeMode: phaseConfigSettings.generationTypeMode,
    setGenerationTypeMode: phaseConfigSettings.setGenerationTypeMode,
  });
  const { mediaEditing, joinWorkflow } = editing;
  const selectedOutputId = output.selectedOutputId;
  const demoteOrphanedVariants = output.demoteOrphanedVariants;

  const generationActions = useGenerationActions({
    state,
    actions,
    selectedShot: selectedShot || {} as Shot,
    projectId,
    batchVideoFrames: frameSettings.batchVideoFrames,
    orderedShotImages: allShotImages,
  });

  const selectedShotIdRef = useRef(selectedShotId);
  selectedShotIdRef.current = selectedShotId;
  
  const updateGenerationsPaneSettings = useCallback((settings: Partial<GenerationsPaneSettings>) => {
    const shotId = selectedShotIdRef.current;
    if (shotId) {
      const updatedSettings: GenerationsPaneSettings = {
        selectedShotFilter: settings.selectedShotFilter || shotId,
        excludePositioned: settings.excludePositioned ?? true,
        userHasCustomized: true
      };
      updateShotGenerationsPaneSettingsRef.current('shot', updatedSettings);
    }
  }, []);

  const shotActions = useShotActions({
    projectIdRef,
    selectedShotRef,
    allShotImagesRef,
    addToShotMutationRef,
    addToShotWithoutPositionMutationRef,
    createShotRef,
    setIsGenerationsPaneLockedRef,
    shots,
    navigateToShot,
    setCurrentShotId,
    updateGenerationsPaneSettings,
    isMobile,
    selectedShot,
  });

  useModeReadiness({
    selectedShot,
    contextImages,
    settingsLoading: settingsLoadingFromContext || false,
    isShotUISettingsLoading,
    isShotLoraSettingsLoading,
    isPhone,
    isMobile,
    generationMode: generationModeSettings.generationMode || 'batch',
    state,
    actions,
    onGenerationModeChange: generationModeSettings.setGenerationMode,
```

```
     8→
     9→describe('timelineWriteQueue', () => {
    10→  afterEach(() => {
    11→    vi.useRealTimers();
    12→  });
    13→
    14→  it('times out stalled writes and wraps the abort as a timeline timeout error', async () => {
    15→    vi.useFakeTimers();
    16→    const onTimeout = vi.fn();
    17→
    18→    const stalledWrite = runTimelineWriteWithTimeout(
    19→      'save-frames',
    20→      (signal) =>
    21→        new Promise<void>((_, reject) => {
    22→          signal.addEventListener(
    23→            'abort',
    24→            () => reject(new DOMException('aborted', 'AbortError')),
    25→            { once: true },
    26→          );
    27→      }),
    28→      { timeoutMs: 10, onTimeout },
    29→    );
    30→    const assertion = expect(stalledWrite).rejects.toSatisfy((error: unknown) => {
    31→      if (!isTimelineWriteTimeoutError(error)) {
    32→        return false;
    33→      }
    34→      return error instanceof Error && error.message.includes('save-frames');
    35→    });
    36→
    37→    await vi.advanceTimersByTimeAsync(11);
    38→    await assertion;
    39→    expect(onTimeout).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    40→  });
    41→
    42→  it('isTimelineWriteActive returns true while a write is active and false after it completes', async () => {
    43→    let releaseWrite!: () => void;
    44→    const gate = new Promise<void>((resolve) => { releaseWrite = resolve; });
    45→
    46→    expect(isTimelineWriteActive('shot-a')).toBe(false);
    47→
    48→    const writePromise = runSerializedTimelineWrite('shot-a', 'test-op', async () => {
    49→      await gate;
    50→    });
    51→
    52→    await vi.waitFor(() => {
    53→      expect(isTimelineWriteActive('shot-a')).toBe(true);
    54→    });
    55→
    56→    releaseWrite();
    57→    await writePromise;
    58→
    59→    expect(isTimelineWriteActive('shot-a')).toBe(false);
    60→  });
    61→
    62→  it('serializes writes per shot so later writes wait for the previous write', async () => {
    63→    const order: string[] = [];
    64→    const onEvent = vi.fn();
    65→
    66→    let releaseFirstWrite!: () => void;
    67→    const firstGate = new Promise<void>((resolve) => {
    68→      releaseFirstWrite = resolve;
    69→    });
    70→
    71→    const first = runSerializedTimelineWrite('shot-1', 'first', async () => {
    72→      order.push('first-start');
    73→      await firstGate;
    74→      order.push('first-end');
    75→      return 'first-result';
    76→    }, onEvent);
    77→
    78→    const second = runSerializedTimelineWrite('shot-1', 'second', async () => {
    79→      order.push('second-start');
    80→      order.push('second-end');
    81→      return 'second-result';
    82→    }, onEvent);
    83→
    84→    await vi.waitFor(() => {
    85→      expect(order).toContain('first-start');
    86→    });
    87→    expect(order).toEqual(['first-start']);
    88→
    89→    releaseFirstWrite();
    90→    const [firstResult, secondResult] = await Promise.all([first, second]);
    91→
    92→    expect(firstResult).toBe('first-result');
    93→    expect(secondResult).toBe('second-result');
    94→    expect(order).toEqual(['first-start', 'first-end', 'second-start', 'second-end']);
    95→    expect(onEvent).toHaveBeenCalled();
    96→  });
    97→
    98→  it('releases the queue when a serialized write times out so subsequent writes can run', async () => {
    99→    vi.useFakeTimers();
   100→
   101→    const first = runSerializedTimelineWrite(
   102→      'shot-timeout',

   104→      async () => await new Promise<never>(() => {}),
```

```
/** Shared video/frame utility helpers. */

const FPS = 16;

// Wan models require frame counts in the form 4N + 1.

export function isValidFrameCount(frames: number): boolean {
  return frames > 0 && (frames - 1) % 4 === 0;
}
```

```
-/**
- * Edge function: complete-task
- * 
- * Completes a task by uploading file data and updating task status.
- * - Service-role key: can complete any task
- * - User token: can only complete tasks they own
- * 
- * POST /functions/v1/complete-task
- * Headers: Authorization: Bearer <JWT or PAT>
- * 
- * SUPPORTS THREE UPLOAD MODES:
- * 
- * MODE 1 (LEGACY - JSON with base64): 
- *   Body: { task_id, file_data: "base64...", filename: "image.png", ... }
- * 
- * MODE 3 (PRE-SIGNED URL - Zero Memory):
- *   Body: { task_id, storage_path: "user_id/tasks/{task_id}/filename", ... }
```

```
1→/**
     2→ * Clip initialization/public entrypoint for Join Clips.
     3→ *
     4→ * Responsibilities kept in this file:
     5→ * - poster preloading
     6→ * - stable export surface for clip manager hooks/services
     7→ *
     8→ * Focused modules:
     9→ * - clipFactory.ts
    10→ * - clipCache.ts
    11→ * - clipPendingActions.ts
    12→ * - clipBootstrap.ts
    13→ */
    14→
    15→export { createEmptyClip } from './clipFactory';
    16→export {
    17→  getCachedClipsCount,
    18→  setCachedClipsCount,
    19→} from './clipCache';
    20→export {
    21→  consumePendingJoinClips,
    22→  applyPendingClipActions,
    23→  type PendingClipAction,
    24→} from './clipPendingActions';
    25→export {
    26→  buildInitialClipsFromSettings,
    27→  padClipsWithEmptySlots,
    28→} from './clipBootstrap';
    29→
    30→/**
    31→ * Warm the browser image cache for the given poster URLs.
    32→ * Returns a promise that resolves when all images have loaded (or errored).
    33→ */
    34→export function preloadPosterImages(
    35→  posterUrls: string[],
    36→  alreadyPreloaded: Set<string>,
    37→): Promise<void[]> {
    38→  const promises = posterUrls
    39→    .filter(url => url && !alreadyPreloaded.has(url))
    40→    .map(
    41→      url =>
    42→        new Promise<void>(resolve => {
    43→          const img = new Image();
    44→          img.onload = () => {
    45→            alreadyPreloaded.add(url);
    46→            resolve();
    47→          };
    48→          img.onerror = () => resolve();
    49→          img.src = url;
    50→        }),
    51→    );
    52→  return Promise.all(promises);
    53→}
    54→
```

```
1→import type { PortionSelection } from '@/shared/components/VideoPortionTimeline';
     2→
     3→export {
     4→  quantizeGapFrameCount,
     5→  calculateGapFramesFromRange,
     6→} from './replaceModeGapMath';
     7→export {
     8→  getDefaultSelectionRange,
     9→  getNewSelectionRange,
    10→} from './replaceModeSelectionPlacement';
    11→export {
    12→  selectionsToFrameRanges,
    13→  calculateMaxContextFrames,
    14→  capContextFrameCountForRanges,
    15→} from './replaceModeFrameRanges';
    16→export type { ReplaceFrameRangeSelection } from './replaceModeFrameRanges';
    17→
    18→export function validatePortionSelections({
    19→  selections,
    20→  videoFps,
    21→  videoDuration,
    22→}: {
    23→  selections: PortionSelection[];
    24→  videoFps: number | null;
    25→  videoDuration: number;
    26→}): { isValid: boolean; errors: string[] } {
    27→  const errors: string[] = [];
    28→
    29→  if (selections.length === 0) {
    30→    errors.push('No portions selected');
    31→    return { isValid: false, errors };
    32→  }
    33→
    34→  if (videoFps === null) {
    35→    errors.push('Video FPS not detected yet');
    36→    return { isValid: false, errors };
    37→  }
    38→
    39→  for (let i = 0; i < selections.length; i++) {
    40→    const selection = selections[i];
    41→    const selNum = selections.length > 1 ? ` #${i + 1}` : '';
    42→
    43→    if (selection.start >= selection.end) {
    44→      errors.push(`Portion${selNum}: Start must be before end`);
    45→    }
    46→
    47→    const duration = selection.end - selection.start;
    48→    if (duration < 0.1) {
    49→      errors.push(`Portion${selNum}: Too short (min 0.1s)`);
    50→    }
    51→
    52→    if (selection.start < 0) {
    53→      errors.push(`Portion${selNum}: Starts before video`);
    54→    }
    55→    if (selection.end > videoDuration) {
    56→      errors.push(`Portion${selNum}: Extends past video end`);
    57→    }
    58→  }
    59→
    60→  if (selections.length > 1) {
    61→    const sorted = [...selections].sort((a, b) => a.start - b.start);
    62→    for (let i = 0; i < sorted.length - 1; i++) {
    63→      if (sorted[i].end > sorted[i + 1].start) {
    64→        errors.push('Portions overlap');
    65→      }
    66→    }
    67→  }
    68→
    69→  return { isValid: errors.length === 0, errors };
    70→}
    71→
```

Perfect. Now I have all the information I need. Let me write up my assessment:

## Assessment: Splitting timelineWriteQueue.ts into Three Files

**Summary: This splitting was UNNECESSARY and counterproductive.**

### Key Findings

**File Sizes:**
- `timelineWriteQueue.ts`: 152 lines (core logic)
- `timelineWriteLogging.ts`: 48 lines (logging utility)
- `timelineWriteTimeout.ts`: 87 lines (timeout wrapper)
- **Total: 287 lines**

**Import Pattern (Critical Issue):**
- **Zero independent imports** from `timelineWriteLogging.ts` or `timelineWriteTimeout.ts`
- Only `timelineWriteQueue.ts` imports them and re-exports them
- All 9 consuming modules in the codebase import exclusively from `timelineWriteQueue.ts`
- The test file also imports from `timelineWriteQueue.ts`

**Evidence:**
```
grep output shows only timelineWriteQueue.ts imports the extracted files:
/src/shared/lib/timelineWriteQueue.ts: imports both modules + re-exports
All other files (8 total):
  - useTimelineFrameUpdates.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - timelineMutationService.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - useTimelineCore.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - usePositionManagement.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - timelinePositionSync.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - timelineTrailingEndpointPersistence.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - useFrameCountUpdater.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - timelineFrameBatchPersist.ts: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
  - test file: from 'timelineWriteQueue'
```

### Why This is Problematic

1. **Unnecessary indirection**: The parent module re-exports both extracted modules just to maintain an interface. This adds cognitive load without benefit.

2. **Violates single-consumer principle**: When extracted files are only consumed by their parent, keeping them together is clearer. The CLAUDE.md refactoring principles confirm: "Extract when >300 lines doing one thing" — this code is only 287 total lines, and the extracted pieces aren't independently useful.

3. **Complicates navigation**: A developer looking for how timeout logic works must follow: `timelineWriteQueue` → `timelineWriteTimeout`, then back to `timelineWriteQueue` to see how it's used. The old single-file structure would be more traversable.

4. **Re-export boilerplate**: Lines 1-17 of `timelineWriteQueue.ts` are pure re-exports:
   ```ts
   import { createTimelineWriteQueueLogger, type TimelineWriteEventHandler } from '@/shared/lib/timelineWriteLogging';
   import { isTimelineWriteTimeoutError, runTimelineWriteWithTimeout } from '@/shared/lib/timelineWriteTimeout';
   export { createTimelineWriteQueueLogger, type TimelineWriteEventHandler } from '@/shared/lib/timelineWriteLogging';
   export { isTimelineWriteTimeoutError, runTimelineWriteWithTimeout } from '@/shared/lib/timelineWriteTimeout';
   ```

### Recommendation

**Consolidate back into a single file.** The extracted modules are:
- Small enough (48 + 87 lines)
- Conceptually cohesive (all part of the timeline write serialization system)
- Only used together by a single parent
- Below the 300-line extraction threshold in CLAUDE.md

This would eliminate the re-export boilerplate, reduce navigation overhead, and keep related logic co-located without sacrificing clarity.

```
+import { getSupabaseClient as supabase } from '@/integrations/supabase/client';
+import { getSupabaseUrl } from '@/integrations/supabase/config/env';
+import {
+  storagePaths,
+  getFileExtension,
+  generateUniqueFilename,
+  MEDIA_BUCKET,
+} from '../storagePaths';
+import { normalizeAndPresentError } from '@/shared/lib/errorHandling/runtimeError';
+export type { VideoMetadata } from './video/videoMetadata';
+export { extractVideoMetadata, extractVideoMetadataFromUrl } from './video/videoMetadata';
+
+const DEFAULT_VIDEO_TIMEOUT_MS = 300000;
+const STALL_TIMEOUT_MS = 30000;
+
+interface VideoUploadOptions {
+  onProgress?: (progress: number) => void;
+  maxRetries?: number;
+  signal?: AbortSignal;
+  timeoutMs?: number;
+}
+
+interface ResolvedVideoUploadOptions {
+  onProgress?: (progress: number) => void;
+  maxRetries: number;
+  signal?: AbortSignal;
+  timeoutMs: number;
+}
+
+interface UploadRequestOptions {
+  file: File;
+  bucketUrl: string;
+  accessToken: string;
+  signal?: AbortSignal;
+  timeoutMs: number;
+  onProgress?: (progress: number) => void;
+}
+
+function resolveVideoUploadOptions(
+  maxRetriesOrOptions?: number | VideoUploadOptions,
+  onProgress?: (progress: number) => void,
+): ResolvedVideoUploadOptions {
+  if (maxRetriesOrOptions && typeof maxRetriesOrOptions === 'object') {
+    return {
+      onProgress: maxRetriesOrOptions.onProgress,
+      maxRetries: maxRetriesOrOptions.maxRetries ?? 3,
+      signal: maxRetriesOrOptions.signal,
+      timeoutMs: maxRetriesOrOptions.timeoutMs ?? DEFAULT_VIDEO_TIMEOUT_MS,
+    };
+  }
+
+  return {
+    onProgress,
+    maxRetries: typeof maxRetriesOrOptions === 'number' ? maxRetriesOrOptions : 3,
+    signal: undefined,
+    timeoutMs: DEFAULT_VIDEO_TIMEOUT_MS,
+  };
+}
+
+function assertNotAborted(signal?: AbortSignal): void {
+  if (signal?.aborted) {
+    throw new Error('Upload cancelled');
+  }
+}
+
+async function getRequiredUploadUserId(): Promise<string> {
+  const { data: initialSessionData } = await supabase().auth.getSession();
+  const session = initialSessionData?.session;
+  if (!session?.access_token || !session.user?.id) {
+    throw new Error('No active session');
+  }
+  return session.user.id;
+}
+
+async function getRequiredUploadAccessToken(): Promise<string> {
+  const { data: sessionData } = await supabase().auth.getSession();
+  const accessToken = sessionData?.session?.access_token;
+  if (!accessToken) {
+    throw new Error('Session expired - please sign in again');
+  }
+  return accessToken;
+}
+
+function wait(ms: number): Promise<void> {
+  return new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));
+}
+
+function getRetryDelayMs(attempt: number): number {
+  return Math.pow(2, attempt) * 1000;
+}
+
+function shouldAbortRetries(error: Error): boolean {
+  return error.message.includes('cancelled');
+}
```

Perfect. Now I have all the data I need. Let me compile my assessment:

## Assessment: Timeline-Utils Decomposition

**VERDICT: Mixed — some justified, some unnecessary noise.**

### File-by-File Analysis

#### `timeline-constants.ts` (6 lines) — **NOISE**
- **Content**: Two constants (`TRAILING_ENDPOINT_KEY`, `PENDING_POSITION_KEY`)
- **Imports**: Only imported by `timeline-dimensions.ts`, `timeline-utils.ts` (5 mentions total across all files)
- **Justification**: Trivial extraction. These constants are part of the core mental model but don't deserve their own file. They should live inline at the top of `timeline-utils.ts` or embedded in `timeline-pairs.ts` which uses them heavily.
- **Cost**: Extra module boundary, one additional import statement per file that needs these constants.

#### `timeline-pairs.ts` (30 lines) — **BORDERLINE / WONTFIX**
- **Content**: Single function `getPairInfo()` that extracts pair metadata from frame positions
- **Imports**: Only by `timeline-utils.ts` and re-exported
- **Usage**: 2 distinct consumers (`useComputedTimelineData.ts`, `usePairSettingsHandler.ts`)
- **Assessment**: Technically under the 30-line threshold for justified extractions, but `getPairInfo` is a **semantically cohesive utility** — it's a single, focused task. Not noisy, but borderline. The extraction is defensible if you view "pair processing" as a distinct concern, though it adds one level of indirection for minimal gain.

#### `timeline-dimensions.ts` (58 lines) — **JUSTIFIED**
- **Content**: Two focused functions (`getTimelineDimensions`, `getTrailingEffectiveEnd`) for computing viewport bounds
- **Imports**: Only by `timeline-utils.ts` and re-exported
- **Usage**: 4 distinct consumers (`useTimelineViewportController.ts`, `TimelineContainer.tsx`, tests)
- **Assessment**: This file is cohesive (both functions compute dimensions for timeline rendering) and imported by multiple modules. The extraction makes semantic sense: "all logic for computing viewport dimensions lives here."

#### `timeline-video-utils.ts` (109 lines) — **JUSTIFIED**
- **Content**: Two focused functions (`calculateNewVideoPlacement`, `findTrailingVideoInfo`) for video-specific timeline logic
- **Imports**: Only by `timeline-utils.ts` and re-exported
- **Usage**: 2+ distinct consumers (`GuidanceVideoControls.tsx`, and potentially others via re-export)
- **Assessment**: This is a **legitimate thematic split**. Video-specific logic (placement, trailing video matching) is distinct from image positioning. The size (109 lines) is substantial enough to justify its own module.

#### `timeline-utils.ts` (192 lines) — **OVERLOADED**
- **Content**: Core positioning functions (`sortPositionEntries`, `quantizePositions`, `applyFluidTimeline`, `applyFluidTimelineMulti`, `pixelToFrame`) plus re-exports
- **Imports**: 19 files import from this, all expecting a unified API
- **Assessment**: This is the **actual problem**. The core file is still doing too much. The 192 lines should be split by concern:
  - Quantization logic (`quantizePositions`) — 35 lines, used by `timelinePositionOperations.ts`
  - Fluid movement (`applyFluidTimeline`, `applyFluidTimelineMulti`) — ~70 lines, used by drag handlers
  - Coordinate conversion (`pixelToFrame`) — ~4 lines, used by drag handlers and zoom

### Summary Table

| File | Size | Consumers | Verdict | Reasoning |
|------|------|-----------|---------|-----------|
| `timeline-constants.ts` | 6 lines | Re-export only | ❌ NOISE | Should be inlined; constants are too fundamental to isolate |
| `timeline-pairs.ts` | 30 lines | 2 (via re-export) | ⚠ BORDERLINE | Single function; defensible but adds indirection for < 30 LOC |
| `timeline-dimensions.ts` | 58 lines | 4+ | ✅ JUSTIFIED | Cohesive theme; multiple consumers; clear responsibility |
| `timeline-video-utils.ts` | 109 lines | 2+ | ✅ JUSTIFIED | Video-specific logic; substantial size; clear separation of concerns |
| `timeline-utils.ts` | 192 lines | 19 | ❌ OVERLOADED | Still mixing quantization, fluid movement, and coordinate conversion |

### Recommendation

**The decomposition was **partially justified** but **incomplete and adds ceremony**:**

1. **Kill `timeline-constants.ts`** — Move those two constants to the top of `timeline-utils.ts` or to `timeline-pairs.ts` (which uses them). No benefit from a dedicated file for 2 constants.

2. **Keep `timeline-dimensions.ts` and `timeline-video-utils.ts`** — These have clear themes and are imported by multiple consumers. The extractions reduced the main file and improved discoverability.

3. **Further split `timeline-utils.ts`** — If the goal was decomposition, it's incomplete. Extract:
   - `timeline-quantization.ts` for `quantizePositions` (used by positioning operations)
   - `timeline-fluid-movement.ts` for `applyFluidTimeline*` (used by drag handlers)
   - Keep `sortPositionEntries` and `pixelToFrame` as helpers or roll them into their consumers

The current state is **file-splitting without a coherent strategy** — some boundaries are meaningful, others are just noise. The central file still has too much responsibility despite the extractions.

```
import {
  authenticateRequest,
  type AuthOptions,
  type AuthResult,
} from './auth.ts';
import {
  initEdgeRuntime,
  operationFailureResponse,
  parseJsonBody,
  parseJsonBodyStrict,
  parseJsonFailureResponse,
  type EdgeRuntime,
  type InitEdgeRuntimeOptions,
} from './edgeRequest.ts';
import { jsonResponse } from './http.ts';

type BodyParseMode = 'none' | 'strict' | 'loose';
type ErrorResponseFormat = 'json' | 'text';

export interface EdgeBootstrapOptions {
  functionName: string;
  logPrefix: string;
  method?: string;
  parseBody?: BodyParseMode;
  corsPreflight?: boolean;
  errorResponseFormat?: ErrorResponseFormat;
  invalidJsonMessage?: string;
  invalidJsonShapeMessage?: string;
  auth?: {
    required?: boolean;
    requireServiceRole?: boolean;
    options?: AuthOptions;
  };
  runtimeOptions?: InitEdgeRuntimeOptions;
}

export interface EdgeRequestContext<TBody extends Record<string, unknown>> {
  req: Request;
  body: TBody;
  supabaseAdmin: EdgeRuntime['supabaseAdmin'];
  logger: EdgeRuntime['logger'];
  auth: AuthResult | null;
}

type EdgeBootstrapResult<TBody extends Record<string, unknown>> =
  | { ok: true; value: EdgeRequestContext<TBody> }
  | { ok: false; response: Response };

function buildErrorResponse(
  message: string,
  status: number,
  format: ErrorResponseFormat,
  extraHeaders?: Record<string, string>,
): Response {
  if (format === 'text') {
    return new Response(message, {
      status,
      headers: extraHeaders,
    });
  }

  const response = jsonResponse({ error: message }, status);
  if (!extraHeaders || Object.keys(extraHeaders).length === 0) {
    return response;
  }

  const headers = new Headers(response.headers);
  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(extraHeaders)) {
    headers.set(key, value);
  }

  return new Response(response.body, {
    status: response.status,
    headers,
  });
}

function parseMode(options: EdgeBootstrapOptions): BodyParseMode {
  return options.parseBody ?? 'none';
}

function methodFor(options: EdgeBootstrapOptions): string {
  return options.method ?? 'POST';
}

function authIsRequired(options: EdgeBootstrapOptions): boolean {
  return options.auth?.required ?? true;
}

function errorFormat(options: EdgeBootstrapOptions): ErrorResponseFormat {
  return options.errorResponseFormat ?? 'json';
}

/**
 * Shared bootstrap for edge handlers:
 * - OPTIONS short-circuit
 * - method guard
 * - runtime initialization
 * - optional JSON body parsing

 */
export async function bootstrapEdgeHandler<TBody extends Record<string, unknown> = Record<string, unknown>>(
  req: Request,
  options: EdgeBootstrapOptions,
): Promise<EdgeBootstrapResult<TBody>> {
  const method = methodFor(options);
  const format = errorFormat(options);
  const corsPreflight = options.corsPreflight ?? true;

  if (corsPreflight && req.method === 'OPTIONS') {
    return {
      ok: false,
      response: jsonResponse({ ok: true }),
    };
  }

  const runtimeResult = initEdgeRuntime(
    options.functionName,
    options.logPrefix,
    options.runtimeOptions,
  );
  if (!runtimeResult.ok) {
    return {
      ok: false,
      response: operationFailureResponse(runtimeResult, 500),
    };
  }

  const { supabaseAdmin, logger } = runtimeResult.value;

  if (req.method !== method) {
    logger.warn('Method not allowed', { method: req.method });
    await logger.flush();
    return {
      ok: false,
      response: buildErrorResponse('Method not allowed', 405, format, { Allow: method }),
    };
  }

  let body: Record<string, unknown> = {};

  if (parseMode(options) === 'strict') {
    const parsedBody = await parseJsonBodyStrict(req, logger, {
      message: options.invalidJsonMessage,
      nonObjectMessage: options.invalidJsonShapeMessage,
    });
    if (!parsedBody.ok) {
      return {
        ok: false,
        response: format === 'text'
```

```
import { createClient } from './supabaseClient.ts';
import { SystemLogger } from './systemLogger.ts';
import {
  type OperationFailure,
  operationFailure,
  operationSuccess,
  type OperationResult,
} from './edgeOperation.ts';

declare const Deno: { env: { get: (key: string) => string | undefined } };

export interface EdgeRuntime {
  supabaseAdmin: ReturnType<typeof createClient>;
  logger: SystemLogger;
}

export interface InitEdgeRuntimeOptions {
  clientOptions?: NonNullable<Parameters<typeof createClient>[2]>;
}

export function initEdgeRuntime(
  functionName: string,
  missingEnvLogPrefix: string,
  options?: InitEdgeRuntimeOptions,
): OperationResult<EdgeRuntime> {
  const serviceKey = Deno.env.get('SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY');
  const supabaseUrl = Deno.env.get('SUPABASE_URL');

  if (!serviceKey || !supabaseUrl) {
    return operationFailure(new Error('Server configuration error'), {
      policy: 'fail_closed',
      errorCode: 'server_configuration_error',
      message: 'Server configuration error',
      recoverable: false,
      cause: { missingEnvLogPrefix },
    });
  }

  const supabaseAdmin = options?.clientOptions
    ? createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceKey, options.clientOptions)
    : createClient(supabaseUrl, serviceKey);
  const logger = new SystemLogger(supabaseAdmin, functionName);

  return operationSuccess({ supabaseAdmin, logger }, { policy: 'best_effort' });
}

export async function ensureRequestMethod(
  req: Request,
  method: string,
  logger?: SystemLogger,
): Promise<OperationResult<void>> {
  if (req.method === method) {
    return operationSuccess(undefined, { policy: 'best_effort' });
  }

  logger?.warn('Method not allowed', { method: req.method });
  if (logger) {
    await logger.flush();
  }
  return operationFailure(new Error('Method not allowed'), {
    policy: 'fail_closed',
    errorCode: 'method_not_allowed',
    message: 'Method not allowed',
    recoverable: false,
    cause: { method: req.method, allow: method },
  });
}

export function requestMethodFailureResponse(
  failure: OperationFailure,
  method: string,
): Response {
  return parseJsonFailureResponse(failure, 405, { Allow: method });
}

export function operationFailureResponse(
  failure: OperationFailure,
  status = 500,
  extraHeaders?: Record<string, string>,
): Response {
  return edgeErrorResponse(
    {
      errorCode: failure.errorCode,
      message: failure.message,
      recoverable: failure.recoverable,
    },
    status,
    extraHeaders,
  );
}

function isJsonObject(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown> {
  return !!value && typeof value === 'object' && !Array.isArray(value);
}

function coerceLooseObject(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> {
  return isJsonObject(value) ? value : {};
}
```

## Assessment: DataFreshnessManager Decomposition

**Finding: This is unjustified decomposition — files are split but not independently used. The extraction creates false module boundaries.**

### Evidence

**Import Pattern (Critical):**
- `queryFreshness.ts` — imported **only** by `DataFreshnessManager.ts`
- `debugGlobal.ts` — imported **only** by `DataFreshnessManager.ts`
- `subscribers.ts` — imported **only** by `DataFreshnessManager.ts`

No other files in the codebase import from any of these extracted modules. They form a private, internal API of the manager.

**File Sizes:**

| File | Lines | Character |
|------|-------|-----------|
| `DataFreshnessManager.ts` | 346 | Main manager logic |
| `queryFreshness.ts` | 49 | 5 utility functions for `lastEventTimes` map |
| `debugGlobal.ts` | 16 | Single wrapper function |
| `subscribers.ts` | 26 | Simple subscriber pattern class |

**The Problem:**

1. **Pure internal utilities, not modules.** `queryFreshness.ts` contains 5 stateless helper functions that operate on the manager's state. They're not separable concerns—they're just extracted helper methods.

2. **No reusability.** These aren't building blocks used by other parts of the system. The subscriber pattern could hypothetically be reused, but it's never imported outside the manager.

3. **Increased indirection without benefit.** The manager now imports from 3 internal modules just to manage its own state. This adds cognitive overhead (three files to read instead of one) without reducing cyclomatic complexity or improving separation of concerns.

4. **Breaks the single-responsibility principle in reverse.** The extracted files aren't "single responsibility"—they're fragments of one responsibility distributed across files.

### Comparison to Valid Extraction

**Valid extraction:** A concern is extracted because:
- It has its own lifecycle (e.g., a worker service)
- Other parts of the system depend on it directly
- It's testable in isolation and used multiple places
- It reduces the primary file below 300 LOC

**This extraction:** None of these apply. It's splitting a 346-line coherent class across files that are never imported independently.

### Recommendation

**Merge back.** Consolidate `queryFreshness.ts`, `debugGlobal.ts`, and `subscribers.ts` into `DataFreshnessManager.ts`. Keep the structure via:
- Comments/sections for clarity
- Private helper methods (e.g., `private markQueryKeysFresh()`, `private registerDebugGlobal()`)
- Private `DataFreshnessSubscribers` class as an inner class if needed

This brings back the 346-line file (well under the 300-LOC guideline when that's a single cohesive behavior, not multiple concerns), improves clarity by seeing the whole manager at once, and removes the false module boundary.

## Analysis Report: New File Extractions on Branch

### Summary Table

| # | File | Lines | Logic Only | Consumers | Verdict |
|---|------|-------|-----------|-----------|---------|
| 1 | `useGenerationsPaneControllerModel.ts` | 282 | ~280 | 1 | **OVER-DECOMPOSED** - Massive hook (282 lines), single consumer; should be inlined into `useGenerationsPaneController.ts` |
| 2 | `AdvancedSettingsLoraModal.tsx` | 54 | ~40 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Small wrapper; single consumer is parent component AdvancedSettingsSection; wraps logic cleanly |
| 3 | `NegativePromptField.tsx` | 52 | ~45 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Reusable field component; extracted from parent for clarity; single consumer is parent |
| 4 | `PromptFieldsSection.tsx` | 84 | ~65 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Self-contained section with conditional render; single consumer is parent |
| 5 | `useAdvancedSettingsHandlers.ts` | 136 | ~130 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Medium-sized hook (9 related callback handlers); single consumer parent but complex enough to warrant extraction |
| 6 | `useAdvancedSettingsState.ts` | 67 | ~60 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - State hook managing 3 derived properties; single consumer is parent; clean responsibility |
| 7 | `useTaskDetailsModalState.ts` | 56 | ~50 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Modal state management (8 state fields + 2 handlers); single consumer parent; focused concern |
| 8 | `TaskPhaseDetails.tsx` | 159 | ~140 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Complex rendering logic (phase/LoRA display); used in 2 places in `VideoTravelDetails.tsx` |
| 9 | `TaskTravelMetadata.tsx` | 103 | ~90 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Self-contained metadata section; single consumer parent |
| 10 | `replaceModeFrameRanges.ts` | 96 | ~90 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Math utilities (frame range calculations); single consumer is aggregator `replaceModeMath.ts` |
| 11 | `replaceModeGapMath.ts` | 35 | ~30 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Pure math functions; single consumer is aggregator; under 40 lines is OK for focused domain math |
| 12 | `replaceModeSelectionPlacement.ts` | 45 | ~40 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Selection placement logic; single consumer is aggregator; ~40 line boundary is acceptable |
| 13 | `clipBootstrap.ts` | 109 | ~100 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Initialization logic (clips + transition prompts + poster preload); single consumer is `clipInitService.ts` (facade) |
| 14 | `clipCache.ts` | 52 | ~45 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - LocalStorage cache operations; single consumer is facade; clean separation of concern |
| 15 | `clipFactory.ts` | 7 | ~5 | 2 | **OVER-DECOMPOSED** - Trivial factory (1 function, 5 lines); used in `clipBootstrap.ts` and `clipInitService.ts`; should be inlined |
| 16 | `clipPendingActions.ts` | 267 | ~250 | 1 | **JUSTIFIED** - Complex pending clips logic (parsing, validation, deferred actions); single consumer is facade; >200 lines of real work |

---

### Strong Candidates for Re-inlining

**1. `useGenerationsPaneControllerModel.ts` (282 lines)**
- **Issue**: This is a massive hook with **single consumer** (`useGenerationsPaneController.ts`)
- **Pattern**: It's not a reusable hook—it's wrapper logic around existing hooks (pane state, gallery state, shots)
- **Why inline**: The file is 282 lines doing mostly wiring; the parent hook just re-exports its return. This adds cognitive overhead without reuse benefit.
- **Cost of change**: Low (one internal import to delete, paste logic into parent hook)

**2. `clipFactory.ts` (7 lines)**
- **Issue**: Trivial function (`createEmptyClip()` just calls `generateUUID()` and returns a shape)
- **Pattern**: Single-export factory used in 2 places, but so simple that the savings don't justify the file
- **Why inline**: The factory is a one-liner; both consumers can call it inline or keep it as a const
- **Cost of change**: Very low (replace 2 imports with the 1-line factory logic)

---

### Justified Extractions (Well-Decomposed)

**SegmentSettingsForm sub-components** (#2–7): All extract meaningful UI sections or state logic from a complex parent. Each is single-consumer because they're specialized parts of `AdvancedSettingsSection`. This is good decomposition for a parent >300 LOC.

**edit-video math utilities** (#10–12): Even though single consumers, these three files are re-exported through an aggregator (`replaceModeMath.ts`), which acts as a public API. This is a clean facade pattern—the math is domain-focused and could theoretically be reused by other edit-video logic later.

**join-clips lib modules** (#13–16): All serve as focused modules behind a facade (`clipInitService.ts`). `clipPendingActions.ts` in particular is substantial (~250 lines of real logic) and deserves its own file. The cache and bootstrap modules are also appropriately scoped.

**TaskDetails components** (#8–9): `TaskPhaseDetails` is used twice in `VideoTravelDetails.tsx` (conditionally in two layout branches), justifying extraction. Both are ~100+ LOC with clear boundaries.

---

### Recommendation

**Action items:**
1. **Inline `useGenerationsPaneControllerModel.ts`** into `useGenerationsPaneController.ts` — it's just a mega-hook wrapper with no reuse.
2. **Inline `clipFactory.ts`** — the `createEmptyClip()` function is a 5-line one-liner; no value in a separate file.
3. **Keep everything else** — the other extractions are either reused across consumers or complex enough (100+ LOC) to warrant separation.

## Comprehensive Assessment of Prop Grouping Changes

Based on my analysis of the three files, here's my detailed assessment:

### 1. SegmentSettingsForm Props

**Before (main):** 39 flat props in a single interface
**After (current branch):** Same 39 props, but split into 6 grouped sub-interfaces that are intersected back together

**Assessment: UNNECESSARY NESTING with NEGATIVE SIDE EFFECTS**

The grouping does NOT reflect domain boundaries effectively:

1. **Grouping adds destructuring overhead without proportional benefit:**
   - SegmentSettingsForm receives grouped props (`defaults`, `structureVideo`, `videoInteraction`) but **immediately destructures them back to flat props** (lines 70-89 in AdvancedSettingsSection):
     ```tsx
     const shotDefaults = defaults?.shotDefaults;
     const hasOverride = defaults?.hasOverride;
     const onSaveFieldAsDefault = defaults?.onSaveFieldAsDefault;
     const {
       type: structureVideoType,
       url: structureVideoUrl,
       // ... 5 more destructures
     } = structureVideo ?? {};
     ```

2. **Immediate re-scattering to children:**
   - AdvancedSettingsSection passes destructured values back to children (PromptFieldsSection, NegativePromptField, StructureVideoSection), not the grouped objects
   - Example (lines 152-166): StructureVideoSection receives flat props like `structureVideoType`, `structureVideoUrl`, not the grouped `structureVideo` object
   - This negates the entire purpose of grouping

3. **Poor domain alignment:**
   - `defaults` groups unrelated concepts: `shotDefaults` (data), `hasOverride` (flags), and `onSaveFieldAsDefault` (callback) are semantically distinct
   - They're together only because they're used in the same section, not because they form a cohesive domain unit
   - NegativePromptField still receives them individually from its parent

4. **Increases cognitive load:**
   - Consumers (AdvancedSettingsSection, StructureVideoSection) must know which group each prop belongs to before using it
   - Then they destructure anyway, defeating the grouping's organizational purpose

### 2. ShotEditor State/Types Props

**Before (main):** ~60 flat props with extensive documentation
**After (current branch):** Same ~60 props split into 7 grouped interfaces

**Assessment: BENEFICIAL GROUPING - CORRECT DOMAIN BOUNDARIES**

This grouping works well because:

1. **Groups reflect real functional domains:**
   - `ShotEditorCoreProps`: identifiers and required context (shotId, projectId, optimisticShotData)
   - `ShotEditorCallbackProps`: event handlers (onBack, onShotImagesUpdate)
   - `ShotEditorNavigationProps`: navigation state/handlers (onPreviousShot, hasNext, etc.)
   - `ShotEditorDimensionProps`: dimension settings as a coherent feature
   - `ShotEditorCacheProps`: cache query functions
   - `ShotEditorRefsProps`: all ref forwarding
   - `ShotEditorUiProps`: UI state (sticky, variant name, drag state)

2. **Groups are passed through as objects:**
   - ShotEditorLayout receives and **passes groups to child sections** (not individual props):
     - `header: { onBack, onPreviousShot, ... }` → HeaderSection
     - `finalVideo: { selectedShotId, projectId, ... }` → FinalVideoSection
     - `timeline: { timelineSectionRef, isModeReady, ... }` → TimelineSection
     - `generation: { ctaContainerRef, ... }` → GenerationSection
   - The grouping is preserved through the call chain (lines 104-166)

3. **Reduces prop drilling:**
   - Parent doesn't need to know which individual props each child needs
   - Child only destructures what it uses
   - If a new prop is added, only the group interface changes, not every call site

4. **Clear separation of concerns:**
   - Size is large (~60 props), so grouping is justified
   - Helps parent understand "what categories of data does ShotEditor need?"

### Summary Table

| Aspect | SegmentSettingsForm | ShotEditor |
|--------|-------------------|-----------|
| **Grouping benefit** | None - groups are immediately destructured | Significant - groups passed through |
| **Domain coherence** | Low - groups are arbitrary (defaults, structure video, video interaction) | High - groups represent functional areas |
| **Prop drilling reduction** | None (re-scattered to children) | Yes (preserved through hierarchy) |
| **Consumer verbosity** | Increased (destructure twice: once to receive, once to use) | Decreased (destructure once per child) |
| **Verdict** | Remove grouping, flatten back to props | Keep grouping as-is |

### Specific Recommendations

**SegmentSettingsForm:**
- The grouping at SegmentSettingsForm level (in the diff) should be **flattened** back to individual props
- AdvancedSettingsSection receives grouped props but immediately destructures them (lines 70-89), which is pure overhead
- NegativePromptField and PromptSection don't receive groups anyway - they get flat props from AdvancedSettingsSection
- The real issue: grouping was applied at the wrong level (at the form entry point) rather than at the section level where it's actually used

**ShotEditor:**
- This grouping is **correct** and should stay
- The pattern of passing groups through the component tree is the right approach for large prop interfaces
- The seven groups have clear semantic meaning that helps readers understand the structure

## Assessment: Edge Function Refactoring Pattern

### Summary
**This is OVER-ABSTRACTION with minimal upside.** The three new shared files (`edgeErrorCode.ts`, `edgeJsonParser.ts`, `edgeRuntimeInit.ts`) extract code that has **zero external consumers** and add unnecessary indirection to already-simple functions.

---

### The Extraction Details

**Before:** All utilities lived in `edgeRequest.ts` (84 lines total):
- `initEdgeRuntime()` - 24 lines
- `ensureRequestMethod()` - 16 lines  
- `readEdgeErrorCode()` - 12 lines
- `parseJsonBody()` and `parseJsonBodyStrict()` - 30 lines
- Helper response functions - 2 lines

**After:** Split across four files:
- `edgeRequest.ts` (84 lines) - now a **re-export barrel** with no logic
- `edgeErrorCode.ts` (12 lines) - single tiny function
- `edgeJsonParser.ts` (83 lines) - JSON parsing with full OperationResult machinery
- `edgeRuntimeInit.ts` (66 lines) - runtime initialization

**Total code: ~245 lines instead of 84.** The extraction added ~161 lines of new imports, exports, and module boundaries.

---

### Real Usage Footprint

**Direct function-level consumers:**
- `readEdgeErrorCode()`: Used in 2 files (`billing.ts`, `edgeHandler.ts`) — both in `_shared/`
- `parseJsonBody()` / `parseJsonBodyStrict()`: Used in 2 files (`complete_task/request.ts`, `edgeHandler.ts`)
- `initEdgeRuntime()`: Used only in `edgeHandler.ts`
- `ensureRequestMethod()`: **Not used anywhere** in the codebase

**High-level consumers:**
- `bootstrapEdgeHandler()` / `withEdgeRequest()`: Used by 13–17 functions across the suite

### The Problem

1. **Zero external abstraction payoff**: The three functions live in `_shared/` but have **only 1–2 internal consumers each**. There's no "multiple independent services" scenario justifying the extraction.

2. **Module bloat**: Instead of "one place to look" for edge request utilities, you now have:
   - `edgeRequest.ts` (barrel file, no logic)
   - `edgeErrorCode.ts` (12 lines)
   - `edgeJsonParser.ts` (83 lines)
   - `edgeRuntimeInit.ts` (66 lines)
   - `edgeHandler.ts` (281 lines)
   
   A developer looking for "how do we parse JSON in edge functions?" must now check 4–5 files.

3. **Re-export boilerplate**: `edgeRequest.ts` is now purely a barrel that re-exports from the other three. This adds cognitive load without removing duplication—developers still write the same import paths, but now there's an extra hop.

4. **Unused exports**: `ensureRequestMethod()` is exported and tested but **never called**. Dead code that was shipped as part of the refactoring.

5. **The actual choreography is untouched**: The refactoring didn't simplify `edgeHandler.ts` or `bootstrapEdgeHandler()`. That function still orchestrates the same 10-step flow; it just imports its dependencies from more files.

---

### What Was Actually Gained?

Looking at the function-level diffs (`claim-next-task`, `stripe-checkout`, `generate-pat`):

**Before:**
```ts
const bootstrap = await bootstrapEdgeHandler(req, options);
if (!bootstrap.ok) return bootstrap.response;
const { supabaseAdmin, logger, auth } = bootstrap.value;
// ... handler logic
```

**After:**
```ts
return withEdgeRequest(req, options, async ({ supabaseAdmin, logger, auth }) => {
  // ... handler logic
});
```

**This is a pattern improvement** (callback-based error handling vs. manual `if/else`), but it has **nothing to do** with the extraction of `edgeErrorCode`, `edgeJsonParser`, and `edgeRuntimeInit`. That refactoring was orthogonal and added noise.

---

### Verdict

**Genuine dedup:** No.  
**Over-abstraction:** Yes, severe.

The extraction violates the "single responsibility + low coupling" principle by splitting one logical module (edge request utilities) across five files with minimal real-world coupling pressure. The three new files each have only 1–2 callers, all within the same boundary (`_shared/`), which is a textbook sign that they don't warrant separate extraction.

**Recommendation:** Inline `edgeErrorCode.ts`, `edgeJsonParser.ts`, and `edgeRuntimeInit.ts` back into `edgeRequest.ts`. Keep the `withEdgeRequest` callback pattern refactoring—that's a real improvement—but revert the file-level splits.

## Assessment Report: Video Utilities Reorganization

### Summary
This is a **pure folder reorganization with minimal value**. The changes move video-specific utilities into a `src/shared/lib/media/video/` subdirectory but add no architectural benefit, introduce a re-export layer, and require consumers to update import paths.

### Files Involved

**Moved to new directory:**
- `videoFrameRate.ts` (5 lines) - Constants only
- `videoPosterExtractor.ts` (133 lines) - Frame extraction utilities 
- `videoThumbnailGenerator.ts` (214 lines) - Thumbnail generation
- `videoUtils.ts` (46 lines) - Frame validation & conversion helpers
- `videoMetadata.ts` (85 lines) - Video metadata extraction (new file)

**Total moved: 483 lines** across 5 files

**No barrel file created** - consumers must use full path imports.

### Consumer Impact

**15 files updated** to change imports from `@/shared/lib/media/videoX` to `@/shared/lib/media/video/videoX`:
- Tools: join-clips, character-animate, edit-video, travel-between-images
- Components: PhaseConfigSelectorModal, SegmentSettingsForm, VideoTrimEditor, etc.
- Hooks: useBackgroundThumbnailGenerator
- Tests: videoPosterExtractor.test.ts, videoThumbnailGenerator.test.ts

### Re-export Layer

`videoUploader.ts` now re-exports from the video subdirectory:
```typescript
export type { VideoMetadata } from './video/videoMetadata';
export { extractVideoMetadata, extractVideoMetadataFromUrl } from './video/videoMetadata';
```

This provides **one aggregation point** but most consumers bypass it and import directly from `video/` subdirectory, making the re-export incomplete and inconsistently used.

### Actual Refactoring

**None.** Files are byte-for-byte identical to the previous version - this is pure relocation:
- `videoFrameRate.ts`: 5 lines → 5 lines (unchanged)
- `videoUtils.ts`: 46 lines → 46 lines (unchanged)
- `videoThumbnailGenerator.ts`: 214 lines → 214 lines (unchanged)

### Value Assessment

**Minimal architectural value:**
1. **Organization justification is weak** - These aren't large standalone modules; they're tightly coupled with video upload/downloader functionality which remains at the parent level
2. **Inconsistency** - Video-specific code is now split: videoUploader.ts at `media/`, video utilities in `media/video/`, but they're heavily coupled
3. **Re-export layer is incomplete** - Most consumers import directly from the subdirectory; the re-export doesn't provide a coherent facade
4. **Cognitive overhead** - Developers must now remember which video-related utility lives where (is it at `media/video/` or `media/`?)
5. **No bundling benefit** - TypeScript and bundlers don't care about folder structure

### Conclusion

This is **folder shuffling without structural improvement**. The changes satisfy a "video utilities are special, let's group them" impulse but don't deliver meaningful benefits:
- The organization doesn't reduce complexity
- Import paths became longer and more fragmented
- The re-export layer doesn't make the boundary clear (15 consumers still import from subdirectory directly)
- No deduplication, refactoring, or logic improvements occurred

**Recommendation**: This reorganization adds friction for minimal gain. The code would be cleaner if these utilities remained at the media root, or if there was a comprehensive barrel file that consistently routed all video-related imports through `media/videoUploader.ts`. As-is, it's half-finished organization that creates confusion.
